 The **Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps** (**IRGC**),[\[a\]](#cite%5Fnote-17) also known domestically as _**Sepah**_[\[b\]](#cite%5Fnote-18) or _**Pasdaran**_[\[c\]](#cite%5Fnote-19) and internationally as **Iranian Revolutionary Guards**,[\[16\]](#cite%5Fnote-20) is a multi-service primary force of the [Iranian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FArmed%5FForces "Islamic Republic of Iran Armed Forces"). It consists of five service branches: [Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FGround%5FForces "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Ground Forces"), [Aerospace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FAerospace%5FForce "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Aerospace Force"), [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FNavy "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Navy"), [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force"), and [Basij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basij "Basij"). It is led by a [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List%5Fof%5Fcommanders%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "List of commanders of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps"), who is appointed by and reports to the [Supreme Leader of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme%5FLeader%5Fof%5FIran "Supreme Leader of Iran").

The IRGC was officially established by [Ruhollah Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhollah%5FKhomeini "Ruhollah Khomeini") as a military branch in May 1979 in the aftermath of the [Iranian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FRevolution "Iranian Revolution"). Whereas the [Iranian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FArmy "Islamic Republic of Iran Army") (the _Artesh_) protects the country's sovereignty in a traditional capacity, the IRGC's constitutional mandate is to ensure the integrity of the [Islamic Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khomeinism "Khomeinism").[\[17\]](#cite%5Fnote-21) Most interpretations of this mandate assert that it entrusts the IRGC with preventing foreign interference in [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran"), thwarting coups by the traditional military, and crushing "deviant movements" that harm the ideological legacy of the Islamic Revolution.[\[18\]](#cite%5Fnote-22)[\[19\]](#cite%5Fnote-notificationofdesignation-23)

The IRGC has approximately 125,000 personnel, as of 2024[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps&action=edit). The main body is the [IRGC Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC%5FGround%5FForces "IRGC Ground Forces"), which focus on maintaining internal order, but have recently moved towards [expeditionary warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expeditionary%5Fwarfare "Expeditionary warfare") also, [projecting power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power%5Fprojection "Power projection") for the Islamic Republic.[\[20\]](#cite%5Fnote-24) The [IRGC Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FNavy "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Navy") is now Iran's primary force exercising operational control over the [Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian%5FGulf "Persian Gulf"),[\[21\]](#cite%5Fnote-globalbearings1-25) serving as a _de facto_ [coast guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast%5Fguard "Coast guard"). The [IRGC Aerospace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC%5FAerospace%5FForce "IRGC Aerospace Force") is responsible for the [Iranian ballistic missile program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic%5Fmissile%5Fprogram%5Fof%5FIran "Ballistic missile program of Iran") and act as the country's [space force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space%5Fforce "Space force").[\[22\]](#cite%5Fnote-:5-26) The [Basij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basij "Basij"), a [paramilitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramilitary "Paramilitary") volunteer [militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militia "Militia") that serves as the IRGC's [auxiliaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auxiliaries "Auxiliaries"), has a further approximately 90,000 active personnel.[\[23\]](#cite%5Fnote-Abrahamian,%5FErvand%5Fp.175-6-27)[\[24\]](#cite%5Fnote-rferl-28) Lastly, the [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force") is a unit specialized in [unconventional warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unconventional%5Fwarfare "Unconventional warfare"), [military intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military%5Fintelligence "Military intelligence") and [clandestine operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clandestine%5Foperation "Clandestine operation"), responsible for arming, training, advising and, in some cases, commanding pro-Iranian [non-state actors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violent%5Fnon-state%5Factor "Violent non-state actor") abroad—such as [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") and the [Houthis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis "Houthis").[\[25\]](#cite%5Fnote-:13-29) It operates a media arm, known as "Sepah News" within Iran.[\[26\]](#cite%5Fnote-30) Currently, the IRGC is designated as a terrorist organization by Albania, Argentina,[\[27\]](#cite%5Fnote-auto-31) Australia, Bahrain, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Canada, Costa Rica, the Dominican Republic, Ecuador, the European Union, Honduras, Iceland, Israel, Liechtenstein, Moldova, Montenegro, North Macedonia, Paraguay, Saudi Arabia, Serbia, Sweden, Trinidad and Tobago, Ukraine, and the United States.[\[28\]](#cite%5Fnote-32)[\[29\]](#cite%5Fnote-33)[\[30\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc20240619-34)[\[31\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc20260129-35)[\[32\]](#cite%5Fnote-Ukraine-36)

Originating as a radical ideological militia, the IRGC has taken on a growing role in nearly every aspect of Iranian politics, [economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic%5Factivities%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIranian%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Economic activities of the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps") (including [energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Fenergy%5Fcrisis "Iranian energy crisis") and [food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food%5Finflation%5Fin%5FIran "Food inflation in Iran") industries) and society. In 2010, [BBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC%5FNews "BBC News") described the organization as a "business empire".[\[33\]](#cite%5Fnote-37) In 2019, [Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters") described it as "an industrial empire with political clout".[\[34\]](#cite%5Fnote-rhafez-38) IRGC's expanded social, political, military, and economic role under [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud%5FAhmadinejad "Mahmoud Ahmadinejad")—especially during the [2009 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%5FIranian%5Fpresidential%5Felection "2009 Iranian presidential election") and the [suppression of post-election protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%5FIranian%5Fpresidential%5Felection%5Fprotests "2009 Iranian presidential election protests")—has led many [Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western%5Fworld "Western world") analysts to argue that it has surpassed even the country's [ruling clerical class](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guardianship%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FJurist "Guardianship of the Islamic Jurist") in terms of political power.[\[35\]](#cite%5Fnote-NYT20090721-39)[\[36\]](#cite%5Fnote-BBC20090721-40)[\[37\]](#cite%5Fnote-abdo-41) In 2026, [Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters") described the IRGC as a "[state-within-a-state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-within-a-state "State-within-a-state")".[\[38\]](#cite%5Fnote-42)

## Terminology

Government organizations in Iran are commonly known by one-word names that denote their function, rather than acronyms or shortened versions. The general public refers to the IRGC as _**Sepâh**_ (سپاه; [Sepoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepoy "Sepoy"); [Sipahi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sipahi "Sipahi")). _Sepâh_ has a historical connotation of soldiers, while in modern Persian it also describes a corps-sized unit. In modern Persian, _Artesh_ (ارتش) is the more standard term for an army.

_**Pâsdârân**_ (پاسداران) is the plural form of _**Pâsdâr**_ (پاسدار), meaning "Guardian", and members of Sepah are known as _Pāsdār_, which is also their title and comes after [their rank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rank%5FInsignia%5Fof%5Fthe%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Rank Insignia of the Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution").

Apart from the name _Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps_,[\[39\]](#cite%5Fnote-43)[\[40\]](#cite%5Fnote-44)[\[41\]](#cite%5Fnote-45) the Iranian Government, media, and those who identify with the organization generally use _**Sepāh-e Pâsdârân**_ (Army of the Guardians), although it is not uncommon to hear _**Pâsdârân-e Enghelâb**_ (پاسداران انقلاب; Guardians of the Revolution), or simply _**Pâsdârân**_ (پاسداران; Guardians) as well. Among the Iranian population, and especially among diaspora Iranians, using the word _Pasdaran_ indicates hatred or admiration for the organization.\[_[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please%5Fclarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")_\]

Most foreign governments and the English-speaking mass media tend to use the term _**Iranian Revolutionary Guards**_ (**IRG**) or simply the _**Revolutionary Guards**_.[\[42\]](#cite%5Fnote-46) In the US media, the force is frequently referred to interchangeably as the _**Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps**_ or the _**Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps**_ (**IRGC**).[\[43\]](#cite%5Fnote-47)[\[44\]](#cite%5Fnote-48)[\[45\]](#cite%5Fnote-49) The US government standard is _Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps_,[\[46\]](#cite%5Fnote-50) while the United Nations uses _Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps_.[\[47\]](#cite%5Fnote-51)

In the emblem of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC)- Seal of IRGC- , the sentence above the [weapon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weapon "Weapon") consists of a segment of the [Quranic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran "Quran") verse from Surah [Al-Anfal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Anfal "Al-Anfal") **—وَأَعِدُّوا لَهُمْ مَا اسْتَطَعْتُمْ مِنْ قُوَّةٍ—**which, according to the translation by [Abdullah Yusuf Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah%5FYusuf%5FAli "Abdullah Yusuf Ali"), reads: "Against them make ready your strength to the utmost of your power."[\[48\]](#cite%5Fnote-52)[\[49\]](#cite%5Fnote-53)

The force's main role is to provide national security. It is responsible for internal and border security, law enforcement, and [Iran's missile forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Air Force of the Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution"). IRGC operations are geared towards [asymmetric warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymmetric%5Fwarfare "Asymmetric warfare") and less traditional duties. These include the control of smuggling, control of the [Strait of Hormuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait%5Fof%5FHormuz "Strait of Hormuz"), and resistance operations.[\[50\]](#cite%5Fnote-janesprofile-54) The IRGC is intended to complement the more traditional role of the regular Iranian military, with the two forces operating separately and focusing on different operational roles.[\[50\]](#cite%5Fnote-janesprofile-54)

The IRGC is a combined arms force with [its own ground forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground%5FForces%5Fof%5Fthe%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Ground Forces of the Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution"), [navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Navy of the Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution"),[\[21\]](#cite%5Fnote-globalbearings1-25) [air force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Aerospace Force of the Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution"), [intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligence%5FOrganization%5Fof%5FArmy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGuardians%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolution "Intelligence Organization of Army of the Guardians of the Islamic Revolution"),[\[51\]](#cite%5Fnote-55) and [special forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special%5Fforces%5Fof%5FIran#Revolutionary%5FGuards "Special forces of Iran"). It also controls the [Basij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basij "Basij") militia, a volunteer-based force with 90,000 regular soldiers and 300,000 reservists. The IRGC is officially recognized as a component of the Iranian military under Article 150 of the [Iranian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FConstitution "Iranian Constitution").[\[52\]](#cite%5Fnote-56) It is separate from, and parallel to, the other arm of Iran's military, which is called [Artesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FArmy "Islamic Republic of Iran Army") (another Persian word for an army). The IRGC is expected to assume control of any Iranian response, especially in the waters of the [Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian%5FGulf "Persian Gulf"), to attacks on its nuclear facilities.[\[21\]](#cite%5Fnote-globalbearings1-25)

### History and structure

The IRGC was formed on 5 May 1979[\[53\]](#cite%5Fnote-ostovar-57)[\[54\]](#cite%5Fnote-58) by [Abbas Duzduzani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbas%5FDuzduzani "Abbas Duzduzani")[\[55\]](#cite%5Fnote-59)[\[56\]](#cite%5Fnote-60)[\[57\]](#cite%5Fnote-61) following the [Iranian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FRevolution "Iranian Revolution") of 1979 in an effort to consolidate several paramilitary forces into a single force loyal to the new government and to function as a counter to the influence and power of the regular military, initially seen as a potential source of opposition because of its traditional loyalty to the Shah.[\[1\]](#cite%5Fnote-Abedin11-1)[\[58\]](#cite%5Fnote-IISS-62) From the beginning of the new Islamic government, the Pasdaran (Pasdaran-e Enghelab-e Islami) functioned as a corps of the faithful. The Constitution of the Islamic Republic entrusted the defense of Iran's territorial integrity and political independence to [the regular military (_artesh_)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FArmy "Islamic Republic of Iran Army"), while it gave the Pasdaran the responsibility of preserving the Revolution itself.[\[59\]](#cite%5Fnote-CFR-63)

Days after Ayatollah [Ruhollah Khomeini's return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhollah%5FKhomeini's%5Freturn "Ruhollah Khomeini's return") to [Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran "Tehran") on 1 February 1979, [Mehdi Bazargan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehdi%5FBazargan "Mehdi Bazargan")'s interim administration established the Pasdaran under a decree issued by [Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khomeini "Khomeini") on 5 May. The Pasdaran was intended to protect the Revolution and its principles, and to assist the ruling clerics in the day-to-day enforcement of the new government's Islamic codes and morality. There were other, perhaps more important, reasons for establishing the Pasdaran. The Revolution needed to rely on a force of its own rather than borrowing the previous regime's tainted units. As one of the first revolutionary institutions, the Pasdaran helped legitimize the Revolution and gave the new government an armed basis of support. Moreover, the establishment of the Pasdaran served notice to both the population and the regular armed forces that the Khomeini government was quickly developing its own enforcement body.[\[59\]](#cite%5Fnote-CFR-63)

Thus, the Pasdaran, along with its political counterpart, Crusade for Reconstruction, brought a new order to Iran. In time, the Pasdaran would rival the police and the judiciary in terms of its functions.

Although the IRGC operated independently of the regular armed forces, it was often considered to be a military force in its own right due to its important role in Iranian defense. The IRGC consists of ground, naval, and aviation troops, which parallel the structure of the regular military. The Pasdaran was "given control of [Iran's ballistic missile program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's%5Fballistic%5Fmissiles%5Fprogram "Iran's ballistic missiles program") in both missile employment and development.[\[60\]](#cite%5Fnote-64)

Also contained under the umbrella of the more conventional Pasdaran, were the Basij Forces (Mobilization Resistance Force), a network of potentially up to a million active individuals who could be called upon in times of need. The Basij could be called to assist in the defense of the country against internal or external threats, but by 2008 was also deployed in mobilizing voters in elections. Another element was the [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force"), a special forces element tasked with unconventional warfare roles and known to be involved in providing assistance and training to various militant organizations around the world.[\[59\]](#cite%5Fnote-CFR-63)

The Pasdaran was closely associated with Supreme Leader Khamenei during his tenure from 1989 to his death in 2026\. Khamenei used the Pasdaran to build support using expropriated state resources. Reportedly he reached "far down into the ranks and appointed new colonels and brigadiers. 'Khamenei micromanages the whole system, so everyone is loyal to him, He is hyperactive. He knows every low-ranking commander and even the names of their children'", according to Mehdi Khalaji of the Washington Institute for Near East Policy.[\[61\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-iran-5-18-2020-65)

The Basij and Pasdaran were instrumental in crushing the [Green Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FGreen%5FMovement "Iranian Green Movement"), and this gave them political supremacy in Iran. According to at least one source (Abbas Milani, the director of the Iranian Studies program at Stanford), the regime "clearly ... believed it was going to lose control, and the IRGC and the Basij saved the day. The result is that the IRGC now has the upper hand. Khamenei knows that without the IRGC he'd be out of a job in twenty-four hours."[\[62\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-NYer-24-5-2020-66)

[Yahya Rahim Safavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya%5FRahim%5FSafavi "Yahya Rahim Safavi"), head of the IRGC since 1997, was dismissed as commander in chief of the Revolutionary Guards in August 2007\. The dismissal of Safavi disrupted the balance of power in Iran to the advantage of conservatives. Analysis in the international press considered the removal of Safavi to be a sign of change in the defense strategies of Iran, but the general policies of the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps are not personally determined by its commander.[\[59\]](#cite%5Fnote-CFR-63)

The Corps have occasionally distributed food aid packages.[\[63\]](#cite%5Fnote-67)

On 16 March 2022, it established a new independent branch called the "Command for the Protection and Security of Nuclear Centres" involved with [Iran's nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5Fprogram%5Fof%5FIran "Nuclear program of Iran").[\[64\]](#cite%5Fnote-68)

### Ideology and intellectual training

The IRGC incorporates ideological and intellectual instruction into its training system alongside conventional military education. This instruction is overseen by specialized ideological-political bodies within the organization and is designed to reinforce loyalty to the Islamic Republic and the doctrine of _[wilayat al-faqih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilayat%5Fal-faqih "Wilayat al-faqih")_ (Guardianship of the Jurist).[\[65\]](#cite%5Fnote-69)

Training programs for IRGC personnel, including those of the Basij militia, combine military instruction with religious and ideological education. These programs include lectures on Islamic theology and political doctrine, aimed at fostering ideological conformity and countering perceived foreign cultural influence. A central institution in this system is [Imam Hossein University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imam%5FHossein%5FUniversity "Imam Hossein University"), which functions as the IRGC’s primary academic and training center. The university oversees curricula, faculty selection, and accreditation for IRGC educational programs, and provides both military and academic instruction.[\[66\]](#cite%5Fnote-70)

In addition, research and conference activities associated with the IRGC and its academic institutions engage with political theory, civilizational discourse, and critiques of Western power. For example, conferences organized by Imam Hossein University have addressed themes such as the "decline of the United States", combining academic references with ideological perspectives. Scholars generally characterize this engagement with intellectual disciplines, including elements of philosophy, theology, and political theory, as instrumental and oriented toward ideological formation, rather than as independent academic inquiry in the conventional sense.[\[67\]](#cite%5Fnote-71)

### Military structure

Main article: [IRGC Provincial Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC%5FProvincial%5FCorps "IRGC Provincial Corps")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Aras-2025_military_drill_%2826%29.jpg/250px-Aras-2025_military_drill_%2826%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Aras-2025%5Fmilitary%5Fdrill%5F%2826%29.jpg)

IRGC Ground Forces conducting training exercises in May 2025

In late July 2008, reports originating that the IRGC was in the process of dramatically changing its structure. In a shake-up, in September 2008 Iran's Revolutionary Guards established 31 divisions and an autonomous missile command. The new structure changes the IRGC from a centralized to a decentralized force with 31 provincial corps, whose commanders wield extensive authority and power. According to the plan, each of Iran's thirty provinces will have a provincial corps, except Tehran Province, which will have two.[\[68\]](#cite%5Fnote-72)

#### Cyber Security Command of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps

Main article: [Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps Cyber Security Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuards%5FCorps%5FCyber%5FSecurity%5FCommand "Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps Cyber Security Command")

In 2007 command for cyber security was established part of cyberdefense of IRGC. It was renamed in 2014, abbreviated GCDC or CIOC.[\[69\]](#cite%5Fnote-73)

* [Advanced Persistent Threat 34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced%5FPersistent%5FThreat%5F34 "Advanced Persistent Threat 34")

#### Joint Staff

Main article: [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Joint Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FJoint%5FStaff "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Joint Staff")

#### Basij

Main article: [Basij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basij "Basij")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Gathering_of_Basij_in_November_2013_%2810%29.jpg/250px-Gathering_of_Basij_in_November_2013_%2810%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gathering%5Fof%5FBasij%5Fin%5FNovember%5F2013%5F%2810%29.jpg)

Gathering of Basij volunteers, 2013

Basij Mustazafin were initially separate organizations but were merged in 1980 into Corps and merged to its land forces since 2008.[\[70\]](#cite%5Fnote-74)[\[71\]](#cite%5Fnote-75)[\[72\]](#cite%5Fnote-76)The Basij is a paramilitary volunteer militia or "plainclothes militia" founded by the order of the [Ayatollah Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayatollah%5FKhomeini "Ayatollah Khomeini") in November 1979\. On 4 November 1979, in an address to the Revolutionary Guards during the [Iran hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%5Fhostage%5Fcrisis "Iran hostage crisis"), Khomeini ordered the creation of an army of "twenty million Iranians" (_Artesh-e bis million_), proclaiming:

> Equip yourself, get military training and train your friends. Give military training to those who are not trained. In an Islamic country, everyone should be a soldier and have military training. ... a country with 20 million young people \[should have\] 20 million riflemen, an army of 20 million"[\[73\]](#cite%5Fnote-77)

This pronouncement and Article 151 of the constitution, which calls for the government to "provide a program of military training, with all requisite facilities, for all its citizens, in accordance with the Islamic criteria, in such a way that all citizens will always be able to engage in the armed defense of the Islamic Republic of Iran," are believed to refer to the Basij.[\[74\]](#cite%5Fnote-USIoP-78) While "Iranian official estimates sometime put their total part-time and full-time strength at more than 20 million", others estimate the Basij as having "a core strength of 90,000, and up to 600,000" (CSIS, 11 January 2018, p. 4); at 100,000 with "hundreds of thousands of additional Basij could be mobilized in the event or an all-out war" (CRS, 23 May 2018, p. 18).[\[59\]](#cite%5Fnote-CFR-63)

The Basij are "the most visible symbol" of the Pasdaran's strength, whose members "can be seen on street corners in every Iranian city".[\[61\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-iran-5-18-2020-65) They are (at least in theory) subordinate to, and receive their orders from, the Iranian Revolutionary Guards and current Supreme Leader. However they have also been described as "a loosely allied group of organizations" including "many groups controlled by local clerics." Currently, the Basij serve as an auxiliary force engaged in activities such as internal security as well as law enforcement auxiliary, the providing of social service, organizing of public religious ceremonies, and as [morality police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5Freligious%5Fpolice "Islamic religious police") and the suppression of dissident gatherings.

#### Quds Force

Main article: [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force")

The elite Quds Force (or Jerusalem Force), sometimes described as the successor to the [Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah%5FMohammad%5FReza%5FPahlavi "Shah Mohammad Reza Pahlavi")'s [Imperial Guards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FImperial%5FGuard "Iranian Imperial Guard"), is estimated to be 2,000–5,000 in number.[\[23\]](#cite%5Fnote-Abrahamian,%5FErvand%5Fp.175-6-27) It is a special operations unit, handling activities abroad.[\[34\]](#cite%5Fnote-rhafez-38)[\[75\]](#cite%5Fnote-ReutersQA-79) The force's main mission is training, directing, and liaisoning with [Iranian proxies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis%5Fof%5FResistance "Axis of Resistance").

#### Aerospace Force

Main article: [Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Qased_SLV_first_launch_02.jpg/250px-Qased_SLV_first_launch_02.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Qased%5FSLV%5Ffirst%5Flaunch%5F02.jpg)

Launch of the [Qased](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qased%5F%28rocket%29 "Qased (rocket)") SLV

The **Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Aerospace Force** or **Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps Air and Space Force**[\[76\]](#cite%5Fnote-80) (IRGCASF; [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian%5Flanguage "Persian language"): نیروی هوافضای سپاه پاسداران انقلاب اسلامی, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization%5Fof%5FPersian "Romanization of Persian"): _niru-ye havâfazây-e sepâh-e pâsdârân-e enghelâb-e eslâmi_, officially acronymed **NEHSA**)\[_[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation%5Fneeded "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] is the [strategic missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic%5Fmissile "Strategic missile"), [air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air%5Fforce "Air force"), and [space force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space%5Fforce "Space force") within the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) of [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran"). It was renamed from the IRGC Air Force into the IRGC Aerospace Force in 2009.[\[77\]](#cite%5Fnote-81) Its 15,000 personnel man around 80 aircraft and operates several thousand short- and medium-range mobile ballistic missiles, including the Shahab-3/3B with a range of up to 2,100 kilometers.

#### Navy

Main article: [Navy of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Navy of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/IRIS_FS313-01_Shahid_Soleimani_in_2022_Sacred_Defence_Week_parade.jpg/250px-IRIS_FS313-01_Shahid_Soleimani_in_2022_Sacred_Defence_Week_parade.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:IRIS%5FFS313-01%5FShahid%5FSoleimani%5Fin%5F2022%5FSacred%5FDefence%5FWeek%5Fparade.jpg)

[FS313-01 Shahid Soleimani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Fcorvette%5FShahid%5FSoleimani "Iranian corvette Shahid Soleimani") in 2022 during the [Sacred Defence Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred%5FDefence%5FWeek "Sacred Defence Week") parade

IRGC started naval operations using mainly swarm tactics and speedboats during "[Tanker War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanker%5FWar "Tanker War")" phase of the Iran–Iraq War.

IRGC Navy and the regular [_Artesh_ Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FNavy "Islamic Republic of Iran Navy") overlap functions and areas of responsibility, but they are distinct in terms of how they are trained and equipped—and more importantly also in how they fight. The Revolutionary Guards Navy has a large inventory of small fast attack craft, and specializes in [asymmetric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymmetric%5Fwarfare "Asymmetric warfare") [hit-and-run tactics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hit-and-run%5Ftactic "Hit-and-run tactic"). It is more akin to a [guerrilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerrilla "Guerrilla") force at sea, and maintains large arsenals of coastal defense and anti-ship cruise missiles and mines.[\[78\]](#cite%5Fnote-The%5FIran%5FPrimer-82) It has also a [Takavar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takavar "Takavar") (special force) unit, called [Sepah Navy Special Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepah%5FNavy%5FSpecial%5FForce "Sepah Navy Special Force") (S.N.S.F.).

#### Intelligence organization

Corps Intelligence directorate are accused of meddling in the [2021 Iranian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%5FIranian%5Fpresidential%5Felection "2021 Iranian presidential election").[\[79\]](#cite%5Fnote-83)

#### Auxilaries

Main articles: [Liwa Fatemiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FFatemiyoun "Liwa Fatemiyoun") and [Liwa Zainebiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FZainebiyoun "Liwa Zainebiyoun")

The IRGC has recruited, funded, and trained two key militias to fight in Syria, Iraq, and Yemen as well as Iranian Balochistan which are led by IRGC commanders and even operate from Iran. These are:

* ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Liwa_Fatemiyoun_infobox_flag.png/40px-Liwa_Fatemiyoun_infobox_flag.png) [Liwa Fatemiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FFatemiyoun "Liwa Fatemiyoun") (a Shia Afghan militia founded in 2013)[\[80\]](#cite%5Fnote-Soleimani-84)[\[81\]](#cite%5Fnote-auto5-85)[\[82\]](#cite%5Fnote-irgcchiefdirects-86)
* ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Liwa_Zainebiyoun_infobox_flag.png/40px-Liwa_Zainebiyoun_infobox_flag.png) [Liwa Zainabiyoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FZainabiyoun "Liwa Zainabiyoun") (a Shia Pakistani militia founded in 2015)[\[83\]](#cite%5Fnote-87)[\[82\]](#cite%5Fnote-irgcchiefdirects-86)[\[84\]](#cite%5Fnote-OSINT-88)

### Size

See also: [List of equipment of the Navy of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List%5Fof%5Fequipment%5Fof%5Fthe%5FNavy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "List of equipment of the Navy of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") and [List of aircraft of the Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List%5Fof%5Faircraft%5Fof%5Fthe%5FAerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "List of aircraft of the Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/IRGC-AF_SRBMs.jpg/250px-IRGC-AF_SRBMs.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:IRGC-AF%5FSRBMs.jpg)

[IRGC Aerospace Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC%5FAir%5FForce "IRGC Air Force") missiles at an IRGC exhibition in [Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran "Tehran"), shows [Shahab-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-1 "Shahab-1"), [Shahab-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahab-2 "Shahab-2"), [Zolfaghar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zolfaghar%5F%28missile%29 "Zolfaghar (missile)") and [Dezful](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dezful%5F%28missile%29 "Dezful (missile)")

The 2020 edition of The Military Balance, published by [IISS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IISS "IISS"), says the IRGC has about 190,000 active personnel and controls the Basij on mobilisation (as much as 40,000 active paramilitary forces).[\[14\]](#cite%5Fnote-IISS2020-15) It estimates the Ground Force is 150,000 strong and the Aerospace Force, which controls Iran's strategic-missile force, has some 15,000 personnel.[\[14\]](#cite%5Fnote-IISS2020-15) The Naval Forces are estimated to size at least 20,000, including 5,000 [Marines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine%5F%28military%29 "Marine (military)").[\[14\]](#cite%5Fnote-IISS2020-15)

## Conflicts

### Iran–Iraq War

Main article: [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War")

The IRGC emerged from the 1980–1988 [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War") as a largely independent organization, which the clerical leadership relied on. The share of the Iranian military budget allocated to the IRGC in 1980 was 7.3%. This increased to 20.3% by 1982, and then almost 50% by 1987.[\[85\]](#cite%5Fnote-92)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/President_Khamenei_visit_an_Iran-Iraq_war_battlefield.jpg/250px-President_Khamenei_visit_an_Iran-Iraq_war_battlefield.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President%5FKhamenei%5Fvisit%5Fan%5FIran-Iraq%5Fwar%5Fbattlefield.jpg)

President [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FKhamenei "Ali Khamenei") visiting a battlefield during the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War"), August 1988

### Lebanon Civil War

Further information: [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982%5FLebanon%5FWar "1982 Lebanon War")

During the [Lebanese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese%5FCivil%5FWar "Lebanese Civil War"), the IRGC allegedly sent troops to train fighters in response to the [1982 Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982%5FLebanon%5FWar "1982 Lebanon War").[\[86\]](#cite%5Fnote-93) In Lebanon, political parties had staunch opinions regarding the IRGC's presence. Some, mainly the Christian militias such as the [Lebanese Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese%5FForces "Lebanese Forces"), [Phalanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kataeb%5FParty "Kataeb Party"), and most of the Christian groups declared war on the IRGC, claiming they violated Lebanese sovereignty. Others, including Muslim militias, were neutral to their presence. Groups such as the [PSP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive%5FSocialist%5FParty "Progressive Socialist Party") and [Mourabiton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mourabiton "Mourabiton") did not approve of their presence but remained silent to preserve political alliances.

### 2006–2009: Lebanon and insurgencies

Further information: [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%5FLebanon%5FWar "2006 Lebanon War")

During the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%5FLebanon%5FWar "2006 Lebanon War"), several Iranian Revolutionary Guards were reportedly killed by Israeli forces in [Baalbek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baalbek "Baalbek"), a town close to the Syrian border.[\[87\]](#cite%5Fnote-94) Israeli officials believe that IRGC forces were responsible for training and equipping the [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") fighters behind the missile attack on the [INS _Hanit_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS%5FHanit "INS Hanit") which left four Israeli sailors dead and seriously damaged the vessel.[\[88\]](#cite%5Fnote-95)

In January 2006, an IRGC [Falcon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault%5FFalcon "Dassault Falcon") crashed near [Oroumieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urmia "Urmia"), about 560 miles northwest of Tehran, near the Turkish border, Iranian media reported. All fifteen passengers died, including twelve senior IRGC commanders. Among the dead was General [Ahmad Kazemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad%5FKazemi "Ahmad Kazemi"), the IRGC ground forces commander, and [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War") veteran.[\[89\]](#cite%5Fnote-96) Gen. Masoud Jazayeri, spokesman for the IRGC, told state radio that both of the plane's engines had failed, its landing gear had jammed, and there was snow and poor visibility at the time.[\[90\]](#cite%5Fnote-97)

On 7 July 2008, investigative journalist and author [Seymour Hersh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour%5FHersh "Seymour Hersh") wrote an article in _[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FNew%5FYorker "The New Yorker")_ stating that the Bush administration had signed a [presidential finding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential%5Ffinding "Presidential finding") authorizing the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA "CIA")'s [Special Activities Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special%5FActivities%5FDivision "Special Activities Division") to begin cross border paramilitary operations from Iraq and Afghanistan into Iran. These operations would be against the [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force"), the commando arm of the IRGC that had been blamed for repeated acts of violence in Iraq, and "high-value targets" in the war on terror.[\[91\]](#cite%5Fnote-98)

In October 2009, several top commanders of the Revolutionary Guards were killed in [a suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%5FPishin%5Fbombing "2009 Pishin bombing") in the [Pishin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pishin,%5FIran "Pishin, Iran") region of [Sistan-Baluchistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistan-Baluchistan "Sistan-Baluchistan"), in the south-east of Iran. The Iranian state television said 31 people died in the attack, and more than 25 were injured. Shia and Sunni tribal leaders were also killed. The Sunni [Baluchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baloch%5Fpeople "Baloch people") insurgent group [Jundullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jundallah%5F%28Iran%29 "Jundallah (Iran)") claimed responsibility for the attack. The Iranian government initially blamed the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates "United States") for involvement in the attacks,[\[92\]](#cite%5Fnote-99) as well as [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%5FArabia "Saudi Arabia"), the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FKingdom "United Kingdom") and later [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan "Pakistan") for their alleged support of Jundallah.[\[93\]](#cite%5Fnote-100)[\[94\]](#cite%5Fnote-101) The United States denied involvement,[\[95\]](#cite%5Fnote-102) but some reports of US assistance to Jundallah during the Bush administration have come from Western sources.[\[96\]](#cite%5Fnote-103) The attacks appear to have originated in Pakistan and several suspects have been arrested.[\[97\]](#cite%5Fnote-104)

### Syria, 2011–2024

Further information: [Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian%5FCivil%5FWar "Syrian Civil War") and [Iranian support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Fsupport%5Ffor%5FSyria%5Fin%5Fthe%5FSyrian%5FCivil%5FWar "Iranian support for Syria in the Syrian Civil War")

Prior to the Syrian war, Iran had between 2,000 and 3,000 IRGC officers stationed in Syria, helping to train local troops and managing supply routes of arms and money to neighboring Lebanon.[\[98\]](#cite%5Fnote-IranBoostY4telegraph-105)

[General Qa'ani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esmail%5FQaani "Esmail Qaani"), commander of the Quds Force, said: "If the Islamic Republic was not present in Syria, the massacre of civilians would have been twice as bad. They had physically and non-physically stopped the rebels from killing many more among the Syrian people."[\[99\]](#cite%5Fnote-106)

Iranian Revolutionary Guard soldiers, along with fellow [Shi'ite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia%5FIslam "Shia Islam") forces from Hezbollah and members of Iran's Basij militia participated in the capture of Qusair from rebel forces on 9 June 2013.[\[100\]](#cite%5Fnote-107)[\[101\]](#cite%5Fnote-108) In 2014, Iran increased its deployment of IRGC in Syria.[\[98\]](#cite%5Fnote-IranBoostY4telegraph-105)

By late 2015, 194 IRGC troops had been killed in Syria; almost all of these soldiers were officers, with several even reaching the rank of Brigadier.[\[102\]](#cite%5Fnote-109)[\[103\]](#cite%5Fnote-110) Additionally, 354 Afghan combatants had died[\[104\]](#cite%5Fnote-111)[\[105\]](#cite%5Fnote-losses-112) who were fighting under the command of the IRGC, as part of the IRGC-equipped and trained [Fatemiyoun Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FFatemiyoun "Liwa Fatemiyoun"), which is part of [Hezbollah Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah%5FAfghanistan "Hezbollah Afghanistan").[\[106\]](#cite%5Fnote-iransafghan-113) Another 21 Pakistanis also died as part of the [Zainabiyoun Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa%5FZainebiyoun "Liwa Zainebiyoun").[\[105\]](#cite%5Fnote-losses-112)[\[107\]](#cite%5Fnote-114)

The Afghan and Pakistani immigrants volunteered to go to Syria in exchange for salaries and citizenship. The Afghans were recruited largely from refugees inside Iran, and usually had combat experience before joining the IRGC; their status as members of the Iranian military is only vaguely acknowledged and sometimes denied, despite the troops being uniformed fighters led by IRGC officers. They were trained and equipped in Iran, paid salaries by the Iranian military, and received state funerals involving uniformed IRGC personnel.[\[106\]](#cite%5Fnote-iransafghan-113) Mid to late October 2015 was particularly bloody for the IRGC, due to them stepping up their involvement in offensives around [Aleppo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleppo "Aleppo"). During this time, 30 IRGC officers, including "three generals, battalion commanders, captains and lieutenants" and "one pilot" were killed in fighting in Syria, as were several Afghan and Pakistani auxiliaries.[\[108\]](#cite%5Fnote-115)[\[109\]](#cite%5Fnote-memri-10-30-15-116)

The fallen included General [Hossein Hamadani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein%5FHamadani "Hossein Hamadani"),[\[110\]](#cite%5Fnote-117) Farshad Hosounizadeh (IRGC colonel and former commander of the Saberin Special Forces Brigade), Mostafa Sadrzadeh (commander of the Omar Battalion of the Fatmiyoon Brigade), and Hamid Mojtaba Mokhtarband (IRGC commander).[\[109\]](#cite%5Fnote-memri-10-30-15-116)

### Iraq, 2014–present

Further information: [Iranian intervention in Iraq (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Fintervention%5Fin%5FIraq%5F%282014–present%29 "Iranian intervention in Iraq (2014–present)") and [International military intervention against ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International%5Fmilitary%5Fintervention%5Fagainst%5FISIL "International military intervention against ISIL")

Two IRGC battalions were reported to be operating in [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq "Iraq") against the [2014 Northern Iraq offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern%5FIraq%5Foffensive%5F%28June%5F2014%29 "Northern Iraq offensive (June 2014)").[\[111\]](#cite%5Fnote-118) The IRGC is considered to be a principle backer of the [Popular Mobilization Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular%5FMobilization%5FForces "Popular Mobilization Forces"), a loose coalition of [Shi'a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a "Shi'a") militias allied with the Iraqi government in its fight against the [Islamic State of Iraq and Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FState%5Fof%5FIraq%5Fand%5FSyria "Islamic State of Iraq and Syria") (ISIS). Major general [Qasem Soleimani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qasem%5FSoleimani "Qasem Soleimani") was an instrumental force in the Iranian ground mission in Iraq against ISIS, purportedly planning the [Second Battle of Tikrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second%5FBattle%5Fof%5FTikrit "Second Battle of Tikrit").[\[112\]](#cite%5Fnote-theg-119)

In December 2014, brigadier general [Hamid Taqavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamid%5FTaqavi "Hamid Taqavi"), a veteran of the 1980–1988 [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Iraq%5FWar "Iran–Iraq War"), was killed by snipers in [Samarra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samarra "Samarra").[\[113\]](#cite%5Fnote-120) In May 2017, Shaaban Nassiri, a senior IRGC commander was killed in combat near Mosul, Iraq.[\[114\]](#cite%5Fnote-121) In December 2019, the [U.S. Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FAir%5FForce "United States Air Force") conducted airstrikes on weapons caches and facilities of the IRGC-sponsored militant group [Kata'ib Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kata'ib%5FHezbollah "Kata'ib Hezbollah"). In retaliation, the group attacked the [U.S. embassy in Baghdad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FUnited%5FStates,%5FBaghdad "Embassy of the United States, Baghdad") in the [Green Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green%5FZone "Green Zone").[\[115\]](#cite%5Fnote-122)[\[116\]](#cite%5Fnote-123)

On 3 January 2020, Soleimani was killed in a [U.S. drone strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%5FBaghdad%5FInternational%5FAirport%5Fairstrike "2020 Baghdad International Airport airstrike") at [Baghdad International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdad%5FInternational%5FAirport "Baghdad International Airport") along with PMF commander [Abu Mahdi al-Muhandis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu%5FMahdi%5Fal-Muhandis "Abu Mahdi al-Muhandis").[\[117\]](#cite%5Fnote-124)

### 2014 Israeli drone shoot down

On 24 August 2014, IRGC forces said that they had shot down an Israeli drone approaching the [Natanz nuclear facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natanz%5Fnuclear%5Ffacility "Natanz nuclear facility").[\[118\]](#cite%5Fnote-Aljazeera%5Fdrone-125)[\[119\]](#cite%5Fnote-BBC%5Fdrone-126)[\[120\]](#cite%5Fnote-Reuters%5Fdrone-127) According to [ISNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FStudents%5FNews%5FAgency "Iranian Students News Agency"), "The downed aircraft was of the stealth, radar-evasive type ... and was targeted by a ground-to-air missile before it managed to enter the area."[\[118\]](#cite%5Fnote-Aljazeera%5Fdrone-125)[\[120\]](#cite%5Fnote-Reuters%5Fdrone-127) The IRGC statement did not mention how they recognized it as an Israeli drone. Israel offered no comment.[\[119\]](#cite%5Fnote-BBC%5Fdrone-126)

### Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752

Iranian authorities initially denied responsibility for the [Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%5FInternational%5FAirlines%5FFlight%5F752 "Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752") incident. However, the IRGC later admitted that the plane had been shot down by mistake.[\[121\]](#cite%5Fnote-128)

The [Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") took "full responsibility" for mistakenly shooting down the airplane with a surface-to-air missile on 8 January 2020.[\[122\]](#cite%5Fnote-129) President [Hassan Rouhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan%5FRouhani "Hassan Rouhani") stated that it had been approaching an IRGC base. According to a senior commander, the plane was mistaken for a cruise missile.[\[123\]](#cite%5Fnote-130)On 17 January 2020, the IRGC were defended by [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FKhamenei "Ali Khamenei") in the Friday sermon. He said that the downing was a "bitter" tragedy and additionally declared that "Iran's enemies" used the crash and the military's admission to "weaken" the IRGC.[\[124\]](#cite%5Fnote-131)

### Hostage Rescue inside Pakistan

On 3 February 2021, IRGC announced that it had conducted an intelligence-based operation inside Pakistani territory to rescue two of its border guards who were taken as hostages by [Jaish ul-Adl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaish%5Ful-Adl "Jaish ul-Adl") organization two and a half years prior.[\[125\]](#cite%5Fnote-132) The Pakistani army denied that Iran had carried out an intelligence operation inside its territory.[\[126\]](#cite%5Fnote-133)

### Involvement in the Russo-Ukrainian war

Further information: [Iran and the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%5Fand%5Fthe%5FRusso-Ukrainian%5Fwar%5F%282022–present%29 "Iran and the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)")

On 21 October 2022, a White House press release stated that [Iranian troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran%5FArmed%5FForces "Islamic Republic of Iran Armed Forces") were in Crimea assisting Russia in launching [drone attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian%5Fstrikes%5Fagainst%5FUkrainian%5Finfrastructure%5F%282022–present%29 "Russian strikes against Ukrainian infrastructure (2022–present)") against civilians and civilian infrastructure.[\[127\]](#cite%5Fnote-134) On 24 November, Ukrainian officials said the military had killed 10 Iranians and would target any further Iranian military presence in Ukraine.[\[128\]](#cite%5Fnote-135) The Institute for the Study of War assessed that these are likely Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps or IRGC-affiliated personnel, as this formation is the primary operator of Iranian drones.[\[129\]](#cite%5Fnote-136)

### April 2024 Iranian strikes against Israel

Further information: [April 2024 Iranian strikes on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April%5F2024%5FIranian%5Fstrikes%5Fon%5FIsrael "April 2024 Iranian strikes on Israel")

On 13 April 2024, the IRGC, in collaboration with the [Islamic Resistance in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FResistance%5Fin%5FIraq "Islamic Resistance in Iraq"), [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah"), and the [Houthis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis "Houthis"), [launched retaliatory attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April%5F2024%5FIranian%5Fstrikes%5Fagainst%5FIsrael "April 2024 Iranian strikes against Israel") against [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") and the [Israeli-occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%5Foccupation%5Fof%5Fthe%5FGolan%5FHeights "Israeli occupation of the Golan Heights") [Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan%5FHeights "Golan Heights") with [loitering munitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loitering%5Fmunition "Loitering munition"), [cruise missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruise%5Fmissile "Cruise missile"), and [ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic%5Fmissile "Ballistic missile").[\[130\]](#cite%5Fnote-Washington%5FPost-2024a-137) It was retaliation for the [Israeli bombing of the Iranian embassy in Damascus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%5Fairstrike%5Fon%5Fthe%5FIranian%5Fconsulate%5Fin%5FDamascus "Israeli airstrike on the Iranian consulate in Damascus") on 1 April,[\[131\]](#cite%5Fnote-138) which killed two Iranian generals.[\[132\]](#cite%5Fnote-139) The strike was seen as a [spillover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle%5FEastern%5Fcrisis%5F%282023–present%29 "Middle Eastern crisis (2023–present)") of the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza%5Fwar "Gaza war") and marked Iran's first direct attack on Israel since the start of [their proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–Israel%5Fproxy%5Fconflict "Iran–Israel proxy conflict").[\[133\]](#cite%5Fnote-140) The attack was the largest attempted [drone strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drone%5Fwarfare "Drone warfare") in history,[\[134\]](#cite%5Fnote-141)[\[135\]](#cite%5Fnote-aljazeera%5F041424-142) intended to overwhelm anti-aircraft defenses. It was the first time since [Iraq's 1991 missile strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991%5FIraqi%5Fmissile%5Fattacks%5Fagainst%5FIsrael "1991 Iraqi missile attacks against Israel") that Israel was directly attacked by a state force.[\[136\]](#cite%5Fnote-143)

### Twelve-Day War

Further information: [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day%5FWar "Twelve-Day War")

On 13 June 2025, Israel launched a "preemptive" strike targeting high-ranking members of the Islamic revolution and Iran's nuclear infrastructure and key nuclear scientists.[\[137\]](#cite%5Fnote-144) The opening attack killed 30 IRGC generals and nine nuclear scientists. The strike, referred to as Operation Rising Lion, launched the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day%5FWar "Twelve-Day War"). [US airstrikes damaged the nuclear facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%5FUnited%5FStates%5Fstrikes%5Fon%5FIranian%5Fnuclear%5Fsites "2025 United States strikes on Iranian nuclear sites") at [Natanz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natanz%5FNuclear%5FFacility "Natanz Nuclear Facility"), [Isfahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isfahan%5FNuclear%5FTechnology/Research%5FCenter "Isfahan Nuclear Technology/Research Center"), and [Fordow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fordow%5FFuel%5FEnrichment%5FPlant "Fordow Fuel Enrichment Plant"). Notable IRGC generals killed during the Twelve-Day War include:[\[138\]](#cite%5Fnote-145)

* [Mohammad Bagheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad%5FBagheri%5F%28general%29 "Mohammad Bagheri (general)"), chief of staff of the armed forces and the second-highest commander after Ayatollah Khamenei.
* [Hossein Salami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein%5FSalami "Hossein Salami"), commander in chief of the Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps.
* [Gholam Ali Rashid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gholam%5FAli%5FRashid "Gholam Ali Rashid"), deputy commander in chief of the Armed Forces.
* [Amir Ali Hajizadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir%5FAli%5FHajizadeh "Amir Ali Hajizadeh"), head of the airspace unit of the Revolutionary Guards.
* [Gholamreza Mehrabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gholamreza%5FMehrabi "Gholamreza Mehrabi"), deputy intelligence chief of the Armed Forces.
* [Mehdi Rabbani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehdi%5FRabbani "Mehdi Rabbani"), deputy commander of operations for the Armed Forces.
* Davood Sheikhian, the commander of air defense.
* Khosro Hassani, the deputy intelligence chief of the aerospace unit.
* [Mohammad Kazemi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad%5FKazemi "Mohammad Kazemi"), the head of the intelligence
* [Hassan Mohaghegh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan%5FMohaghegh "Hassan Mohaghegh"), the deputy to general Kazemi.
