See also: [Sanctions against Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions%5Fagainst%5FIran "Sanctions against Iran"), [Criticism of the Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIran%5Fnuclear%5Fdeal "Criticism of the Iran nuclear deal"), and [Political repression in the Islamic Republic of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political%5Frepression%5Fin%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRepublic%5Fof%5FIran "Political repression in the Islamic Republic of Iran")

As an elite group, members of Pasdaran have an oversized influence in Iran's political world. [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud%5FAhmadinejad "Mahmoud Ahmadinejad") (President 2005–2013) joined the IRGC in 1985, serving first in military operation in Iraqi Kurdistan before leaving the front line to take charge of logistics. A majority of his first cabinet consisted of IRGC veterans.[\[139\]](#cite%5Fnote-146) Nearly one third of the members elected to Iran's [Majlis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FConsultative%5FAssembly "Islamic Consultative Assembly") in 2004 are also "_Pásdárán_".[\[140\]](#cite%5Fnote-147) Others have been appointed as ambassadors, mayors, provincial governors and senior bureaucrats.[\[75\]](#cite%5Fnote-ReutersQA-79) However, IRGC veteran status does not imply a single viewpoint.[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148)

Strengthening the power of the IRGC was their actions against the [Green Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FGreen%5FMovement "Iranian Green Movement"), where thousands of Iranians protested election irregularities in the 2009 victory of Mahmoud Ahmadinejad over "a well-liked" reformer [Mir-Hossein Mousavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir-Hossein%5FMousavi "Mir-Hossein Mousavi"). As "the demonstrations gained strength, the security forces swept in, arresting, beating, and killing protesters". The IRGC was thought to be crucial in crushing the movement which "marked a turning point" for the Islamic Republic.[\[62\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-NYer-24-5-2020-66)

In a video leaked to the internet, then-leader of the Pasdaran, general [Mohammad Ali Jafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad%5FAli%5FJafari "Mohammad Ali Jafari"), opposed the protest for challenging 'the tenets of the revolution', but warned that it 'was a blow that weakened the fundamental pillars of the regime,' and demonstrated that Iran's rulers "could no longer count on popular support". He added, "Anyone who refuses to understand these new conditions will not be successful".[\[62\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-NYer-24-5-2020-66)

Ayatollah Khomeini urged that the country's military forces should remain unpoliticized. However, the constitution, in Article 150, defines the IRGC as the "guardian of the Revolution and of its achievements" which is at least partly a political mission. His original views have therefore been the subject of debate. Supporters of the Basiji have argued for politicization, while reformists, moderates and [Hassan Khomeini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan%5FKhomeini "Hassan Khomeini") opposed it. President Rafsanjani forced military professionalization and ideological deradicalization on the IRGC to curb its political role, but the Pasdaran became natural allies of Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei when reformists threatened him.[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148) The IRGC grew stronger under president Ahmedinejad, and assumed formal command of the [Basiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basiji "Basiji") militia in early 2009.[\[142\]](#cite%5Fnote-Daragahi-149)

Although never explicitly endorsing or affiliating themselves with any political parties, the [Alliance of Builders of Islamic Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance%5Fof%5FBuilders%5Fof%5FIslamic%5FIran "Alliance of Builders of Islamic Iran") (or Abadgaran), is widely viewed as a political front for the Revolutionary Guards. Many former members (including Ahmadinejad) have joined this party in recent years and the Revolutionary Guards have reportedly given them financial support.

During the [2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fwar "2026 Iran war") the IRGC became the dominant power in Iran, as [Ahmad Vahidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad%5FVahidi "Ahmad Vahidi"), commander of the IRGC, insisted that all critical and sensitive leadership positions must be decided by the IRGC.[\[143\]](#cite%5Fnote-150)[\[144\]](#cite%5Fnote-151)

### Economic activity

See also: [Economy of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy%5Fof%5FIran "Economy of Iran"), [Smuggling in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour%5Fand%5Ftax%5Flaws%5Fin%5FIran#Smuggling "Labour and tax laws in Iran"), and [Economic activities of the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic%5Factivities%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIranian%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Economic activities of the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps")

IRGC first expanded into commercial activity through informal social networking of veterans and former officials. IRGC officials confiscated assets of many refugees who had fled Iran after the fall of [Abolhassan Banisadr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolhassan%5FBanisadr "Abolhassan Banisadr")'s government. It is now a vast [conglomerate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic%5Factivities%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIranian%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Economic activities of the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corps"), controlling Iran's missile batteries and nuclear program but also a multibillion-dollar business empire reaching almost all economic sectors.[\[35\]](#cite%5Fnote-NYT20090721-39) Estimates of the fraction of Iran's economy that it controls through a series of subsidiaries and trusts[\[145\]](#cite%5Fnote-152) vary from 10%[\[146\]](#cite%5Fnote-Ganji-153) to over 50%.[\[61\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-iran-5-18-2020-65)

The _[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los%5FAngeles%5FTimes "Los Angeles Times")_ estimates that IRGC has ties to over 100 companies, with its annual revenue exceeding $12 billion in business and construction.[\[147\]](#cite%5Fnote-154) The IRGC has been awarded billions of dollars in contracts in the [oil, gas and petrochemical industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry%5Fof%5FPetroleum%5Fof%5FIran "Ministry of Petroleum of Iran") as well as major infrastructure projects.[\[148\]](#cite%5Fnote-155)

The following commercial entities have been named by the United States as owned or controlled by the IRGC and its leaders.[\[149\]](#cite%5Fnote-USDT20071025-156)

* [Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khatam%5Fal-Anbiya%5FConstruction%5FHeadquarters "Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters"), the IRGC's major engineering arm & one of Iran's largest contractors employing about 25,000 engineers and staff on military (70%) and non-military (30%) projects[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148) worth over $7 billion in 2006.[\[149\]](#cite%5Fnote-USDT20071025-156)
* [Oriental Oil Kish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oriental%5FOil%5FKish "Oriental Oil Kish") (oil and gas industry)[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* [Ghorb Nooh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghorb%5FNooh "Ghorb Nooh")[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* Sahel Consultant Engineering[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* [Ghorb Karbala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghorb%5FKarbala "Ghorb Karbala")[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* Sepasad Engineering Co. (excavation and tunnel construction)[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* [Omran Sahel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omran%5FSahel "Omran Sahel")[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* [Hara Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hara%5FCompany?action=edit&redlink=1 "Hara Company (page does not exist)") (excavation and tunnel construction)[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* Gharargahe Sazandegi Ghaem[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157)
* [Imensazan Consultant Engineers Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imensazan%5FConsultant%5FEngineers%5FInstitute "Imensazan Consultant Engineers Institute") (subsidiary of Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters)
* [Fater Engineering Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fater%5FEngineering%5FInstitute "Fater Engineering Institute") (subsidiary of Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters)

In September 2009, the [government of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government%5Fof%5FIran "Government of Iran") sold 51% of the shares of the [Telecommunication Company of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication%5FCompany%5Fof%5FIran "Telecommunication Company of Iran") to the [Mobin Trust Consortium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobin%5FTrust%5FConsortium "Mobin Trust Consortium") (Etemad-e-Mobin), a group affiliated with the Guards, for $7.8 billion. This was the largest transaction on the [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran%5FStock%5FExchange "Tehran Stock Exchange") in history.[\[151\]](#cite%5Fnote-158)[\[152\]](#cite%5Fnote-159) IRGC also owns 45% participation in the automotive [Bahman Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahman%5FGroup "Bahman Group") and has a majority stake in Iran's naval giant [SADRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SADRA "SADRA") through [Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khatam%5Fal-Anbiya%5FConstruction%5FHeadquarters "Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters").[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148)[\[153\]](#cite%5Fnote-160)

The IRGC also exerts influence over _[bonyads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonyad "Bonyad")_, wealthy, non-governmental ostensibly charitable foundations controlled by key clerics. The pattern of revolutionary foundations mimics the style of informal and extralegal economic networks from the time of the shah. Their development started in the early 1990s, gathered pace over the next decade, and accelerated even more with many lucrative [no-bid contracts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-bid%5Fcontract "No-bid contract") from the Ahmadinejad presidency.[\[150\]](#cite%5Fnote-Congress-157) The IRGC exerts informal, but real, influence over many such organizations including:

* [Mostazafan Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostazafan%5FFoundation "Mostazafan Foundation") (Foundation of the Oppressed or The Mostazafan Foundation)
* [Bonyad Shahid va Omur-e Janbazan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonyad%5FShahid%5Fva%5FOmur-e%5FJanbazan "Bonyad Shahid va Omur-e Janbazan") (Foundation of Martyrs and Veterans Affairs)[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Sacred_Defence_Week_parade%2C_2023%2C_in_Qom_%28048%29.jpg/250px-Sacred_Defence_Week_parade%2C_2023%2C_in_Qom_%28048%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sacred%5FDefence%5FWeek%5Fparade,%5F2023,%5Fin%5FQom%5F%28048%29.jpg)

Military parade in Qom, Iran, commemorating [Sacred Defence Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred%5FDefence%5FWeek "Sacred Defence Week"), 22 September 2023

As an elite force with great economic assets it has developed into what some observers call an "untouchable élite" and somewhat isolated in Iranian society. According to a "former senior Middle Eastern intelligence officer", the Guard and their families "have their own schools, their own markets, their own neighborhoods, their own resorts. The neighborhoods look like a carbon copy of Beverly Hills."[\[61\]](#cite%5Fnote-Filkins-iran-5-18-2020-65)

Former Bank Ansar and Bank Mehr Iranian were run by [IRGC Cooperation Bonyad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC%5FCooperation%5FBonyad "IRGC Cooperation Bonyad") until their merger with state Bank Sepah.[\[154\]](#cite%5Fnote-161)

In 2023, Israel seized millions of dollars in cryptocurrency belonging to Hezbollah and the IRGC's [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5Fforce "Quds force").[\[155\]](#cite%5Fnote-162)

Between 2023 and 2025, Iran significantly increased its military budget, with a substantial portion directed to the IRGC. In 2023, Iran's military expenditure was estimated at $10.3 billion, with the IRGC receiving approximately 37%, approximately $3.8 billion. By 2024, the total military budget rose to approximately $16.7 billion, with $10.9 billion officially allocated to military entities and an additional $5.9 billion channeled through oil revenues and the National Development Fund. In 2025, the Iranian government proposed a 200% increase in the military budget, allocating over half of its oil and gas export revenues—estimated at €12 billion—to the armed forces, including the IRGC. This surge in funding underscores Iran's focus on enhancing its military capabilities amid escalating regional tensions.[\[156\]](#cite%5Fnote-163)[\[157\]](#cite%5Fnote-164)

### Environmental

Main article: [Tehran water shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran%5Fwater%5Fshortage "Tehran water shortage")

Since the 1979 Islamic Revolution, Iran's pursuit of food self-sufficiency has driven a state-led campaign of agricultural expansion that has severely strained the country's limited water resources. This push, heavily reliant on water-intensive crops such as wheat, rice, and sugar beet, has often ignored ecological constraints. With agriculture accounting for approximately 92% of water usage and among the lowest levels of water productivity in the region, Iran's water crisis is deeply entwined with governance failures, ideological policies, and entrenched institutional interests.[\[158\]](#cite%5Fnote-165)

The Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps, who holds a deep entanglement in Iran's economic, infrastructural, and environmental governance, has had a significant effect on Iran's environmental standing. Through its engineering arm, [Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khatam%5Fal-Anbiya%5FConstruction%5FHeadquarters "Khatam al-Anbiya Construction Headquarters") (KAA), the IRGC has been a principal actor in Iran's dam-building spree, water transfer schemes, and large-scale infrastructure projects. Peripheral provinces such as Khuzestan, which are home to marginalized ethnic communities, have suffered disproportionately from these ecological disruptions.[\[159\]](#cite%5Fnote-166)

The IRGC and its affiliated economic networks, sometimes referred to as the "water mafia," have profited immensely from this system. While the official rhetoric remains fixated on sovereignty and resistance economics, the practical outcome has been a politicized water economy marked by mismanagement and corruption. The Majlis Research Center itself acknowledged in 2023 that the current model of water governance is incompatible with the country's hydrological realities.[\[160\]](#cite%5Fnote-167)

### Analysis

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps_Ground_Force_in_Kerman_tactical_exercise_%28046%29.jpg/250px-Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps_Ground_Force_in_Kerman_tactical_exercise_%28046%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps%5FGround%5FForce%5Fin%5FKerman%5Ftactical%5Fexercise%5F%28046%29.jpg)

Tactical exercise of the IRGC Ground Force in [Kerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerman "Kerman"), Iran, 21 November 2023

[Mehdi Khalaji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehdi%5FKhalaji "Mehdi Khalaji") of the [Washington Institute for Near East Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington%5FInstitute%5Ffor%5FNear%5FEast%5FPolicy "Washington Institute for Near East Policy") argues that the IRGC is "the spine of the current political structure and a major player in the Iranian economy."[\[161\]](#cite%5Fnote-Khalaji12-168) The once theocratic state has evolved into a [garrison state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garrison%5Fstate "Garrison state"), like [Burma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burma "Burma"), whereby the military dominates social, cultural, political, and economic life, protecting the government from internal rather than external opponents.[\[161\]](#cite%5Fnote-Khalaji12-168)

Greg Bruno and Jayshree Bajoria of the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council%5Fon%5FForeign%5FRelations "Council on Foreign Relations") agree, stating that the IRGC has expanded well beyond its mandate and into a "socio-military-political-economic force" that deeply penetrates Iran's power structure.[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169) "The Guards' involvement in politics has grown to unprecedented levels since 2004, when IRGC won at least 16 percent of the 290 seats" in the [Islamic Consultative Assembly of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FConsultative%5FAssembly%5Fof%5FIran "Islamic Consultative Assembly of Iran").[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169) During the elections of March 2008, IRGC veterans won 182 out of 290 seats, helping [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud%5FAhmadinejad "Mahmoud Ahmadinejad") consolidate power.[\[163\]](#cite%5Fnote-FOOTNOTEAlfoneh20083-170)

Half of Ahmadinejad's cabinet was composed of former IRGC officers while several others were appointed to provincial governorships.[\[163\]](#cite%5Fnote-FOOTNOTEAlfoneh20083-170)

Ali Alfoneh of the [American Enterprise Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American%5FEnterprise%5FInstitute "American Enterprise Institute") contends that "While the presence of former IRGC officers in the cabinet is not a new phenomenon, their numbers under Ahmadinejad—they occupy nine of the twenty-one ministry portfolios—are unprecedented."[\[164\]](#cite%5Fnote-FOOTNOTEAlfoneh20086-171) Additionally, Ahmadinejad successfully purged provincial governorships of Rafsanjani and Khatami supporters and replaced them not only with IRGC members, but also members of the [Basij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basij "Basij") and the Islamic Republic prison administration.[\[165\]](#cite%5Fnote-FOOTNOTEAlfoneh20087-172)

The IRGC chief, General [Mohammad Ali Jafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad%5FAli%5FJafari "Mohammad Ali Jafari"), announced that the Guards would go through internal restructuring in order to counter "internal threats to the Islamic Republic."[\[163\]](#cite%5Fnote-FOOTNOTEAlfoneh20083-170) [Bruce Riedel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce%5FRiedel "Bruce Riedel"), a Senior Fellow at the [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings%5FInstitution "Brookings Institution") and former [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA "CIA") analyst, argues the Guards was created to protect the government against a possible coup.[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Great_Prophet_XVII_-_Ballistic_missile_tests_%2803%29.jpg/250px-Great_Prophet_XVII_-_Ballistic_missile_tests_%2803%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Great%5FProphet%5FXVII%5F-%5FBallistic%5Fmissile%5Ftests%5F%2803%29.jpg)

IRGC launching [ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballistic%5Fmissile "Ballistic missile") during the final stage of the "Great Prophet 17" joint military exercise, 24 December 2021

Since the disputed 2009 presidential elections, debate over how powerful the IRGC is has reemerged. Danielle Pletka and Ali Alfoneh see the irreversible militarization of Iran's government.[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169) Abbas Milani, director of Iranian Studies at [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford%5FUniversity "Stanford University"), believes the Guards' power actually exceeds that of Supreme Leader Ayatollah [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FKhamenei "Ali Khamenei").[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169) [Frederic Wehrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederic%5FWehrey "Frederic Wehrey"), adjunct Senior Fellow at the [RAND Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAND%5FCorporation "RAND Corporation") believes the Revolutionary Guards is not a cohesive unit of similar-minded conservatives but rather a factionalized institution that is hardly bent on overthrowing their masters.[\[162\]](#cite%5Fnote-Bruno-169)

### U.S. Department of the Treasury terrorist aid claims

The U.S. Department of the Treasury claims the Corp has supported several organizations the U.S. deems to be terrorist, including [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah"), [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas"), [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian%5FIslamic%5FJihad "Palestinian Islamic Jihad") (PIJ), the [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine – General Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular%5FFront%5Ffor%5Fthe%5FLiberation%5Fof%5FPalestine%5F–%5FGeneral%5FCommand "Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine – General Command") (PFLP-GC), and the [Taliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taliban "Taliban").[\[166\]](#cite%5Fnote-tg810-173) In the U.S. Department of the Treasury's report, four IRGC senior officials, Hushang Alladad, Hossein Musavi, Hasan Mortezavi, and Mohammad Reza Zahedi, were specifically named for providing support to terrorist organizations. Hushang Alladad, a financial officer for the IRGC, was cited as personally administering financial support to terrorist groups including Hizballah, Hamas, and PIJ.[\[166\]](#cite%5Fnote-tg810-173)

Both General Hossein Musavi and Colonel Hasan Mortevazi were claimed to have provided financial and material support to the Taliban. Mohammad Reza Zahedi, the IRGC commander in Lebanon, was claimed to have played a crucial role in Iran's aid to Hizballah. According to the U.S. Department of the Treasury, Zahedi served as a liaison to Hizballah and Syrian intelligence services as well as taking part in weapon deals involving Hizballah.[\[166\]](#cite%5Fnote-tg810-173)

The U.S. Treasury report goes on to detail the IRGC's methods of support for terrorist groups: "The Government of Iran also uses the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) and IRGC-QF to implement its foreign policy goals, including, but not limited to, seemingly legitimate activities that provide cover for intelligence operations and support to terrorist and insurgent groups. These activities include economic investment, reconstruction, and other types of aid to Iraq, Afghanistan, and Lebanon, implemented by companies and institutions that act for or on behalf of, or are owned or controlled by the IRGC and the Iranian government."[\[166\]](#cite%5Fnote-tg810-173)

### Mass media

During the Iran-Iraq War, the IRGC developed an extensive media and cultural production apparatus as part of its broader ideological activities. Contemporary reports indicated that as much as "90 percent" of its non-combat operations were devoted to such "cultural activities", aimed at promoting the religious and political values endorsed by the organization and allied clerical figures. IRGC units organized the production and distribution of both textual and audio-visual materials. According to internal reports from the early 1980s, the organization established over 2,400 libraries across major provinces. Within these and existing libraries, approximately 629,102 books were donated, and a further 482,000 books were distributed nationwide. In addition, IRGC units produced around 440,000 publications, excluding political journals. While many of these materials were distributed free of charge, others were sold, generating approximately 9.75 million rials in revenue, including 3 million rials from sales in southwestern provinces affected by the war. Some ideological and political publications were also distributed to "liberation fronts" outside Iran. The IRGC also invested in audio-visual and artistic production. This included organizing 4,049 film screenings and producing 85 theatrical performances. Provincial units distributed nearly 60,000 audio cassettes covering religious themes, including approximately 6,000 excerpts from the Qur'an and 3,000 recordings related to _Guardianship of the Jurist_. The most widely distributed cassette, numbering 21,000 copies, featured sermons by [Ayatollah Morteza Motahhari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morteza%5FMotahhari "Morteza Motahhari"). These materials were disseminated through local bases and public exhibitions; in total, 1,620 exhibitions were held, where books and cassettes were sold and accompanied by slideshow presentations.[\[167\]](#cite%5Fnote-174)

#### Corporations in media

* [Owj Arts and Media Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Owj%5FArts%5Fand%5FMedia%5FOrganization "Owj Arts and Media Organization"), [Tasnim news agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasnim%5Fnews%5Fagency "Tasnim news agency")[\[168\]](#cite%5Fnote-175)[\[169\]](#cite%5Fnote-176)

### Similar organizations

Since November 2023, [the military of Islamic Emirate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghan%5FArmed%5FForces "Afghan Armed Forces") have created [Supreme leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme%5FLeader%5Fof%5FAfghanistan "Supreme Leader of Afghanistan")\-led task forces in Afghanistan similar to the Revolutionary Guard.[\[170\]](#cite%5Fnote-177)

## Controversy

Main articles: [Controversies surrounding the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controversies%5Fsurrounding%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Controversies surrounding the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") and [Fuel smuggling in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%5FGasoline%5FRationing%5FPlan%5Fin%5FIran#Fuel%5Fsmuggling "2007 Gasoline Rationing Plan in Iran")

From its origin as an ideologically driven militia, the IRGC has taken an ever more assertive role in virtually every aspect of Iranian society. Its part in suppressing dissent has led many analysts to describe the events surrounding the 12 June 2009 presidential election as a military coup, and the IRGC as an authoritarian military security government for which its [Shiite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiite "Shiite") clerical system is no more than a facade.[\[35\]](#cite%5Fnote-NYT20090721-39)

Since its establishment, IRGC has been involved in many economic and military activities, of which some raised controversies. The organization has been accused of smuggling (including importing illegal alcoholic beverages, cigarettes and satellite dishes, into Iran via jetties not supervised by the Government[\[141\]](#cite%5Fnote-RAND821-148)[\[171\]](#cite%5Fnote-178)[\[172\]](#cite%5Fnote-179)[\[173\]](#cite%5Fnote-Erlich-180)), training and supplying [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah")[\[174\]](#cite%5Fnote-181)[\[175\]](#cite%5Fnote-182) and [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas")[\[176\]](#cite%5Fnote-183) fighters, and of being involved in the [Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%5FWar "Iraq War").[\[177\]](#cite%5Fnote-184)

In December 2009, evidence uncovered during an investigation by the Guardian newspaper and Guardian Films linked the IRGC to the kidnappings of 5 Britons from a government ministry building in Baghdad in 2007\. Three of the hostages, Jason Creswell, Jason Swindlehurst and Alec Maclachlan, were killed. Alan Mcmenemy's body was never found but Peter Moore was released on 30 December 2009\. The investigation uncovered evidence that Moore, 37, a computer expert from [Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln,%5FEngland "Lincoln, England") was targeted because he was installing a system for the Iraqi Government that would show how a vast amount of international aid was diverted to Iran's militia groups in Iraq.[\[178\]](#cite%5Fnote-185)

According to [Geneive Abdo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneive%5FAbdo "Geneive Abdo"), IRGC members were appointed "as ambassadors, mayors, cabinet ministers, and high-ranking officials at state-run economic institutions" during the administration of president Ahmadinejad.[\[37\]](#cite%5Fnote-abdo-41) Appointments in 2009 by Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FKhamenei "Ali Khamenei") have given "hard-liners" in the guard "unprecedented power" and included "some of the most feared and brutal men in Iran."[\[37\]](#cite%5Fnote-abdo-41)

In May 2019, the United States accused the IRGC of being "directly responsible" for an [attack on commercial ships in the Gulf of Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May%5F2019%5FGulf%5Fof%5FOman%5Fincident "May 2019 Gulf of Oman incident"). [Michael M. Gilday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael%5FM.%5FGilday "Michael M. Gilday"), United States [director of the Joint Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Director%5Fof%5Fthe%5FJoint%5FStaff "Director of the Joint Staff"), described US intelligence attributing that the IRGC used [limpet mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limpet%5Fmines "Limpet mines") to attack four [oil tankers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil%5Ftankers "Oil tankers") anchored in the [Gulf of Oman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf%5Fof%5FOman "Gulf of Oman") for [bunkering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunkering "Bunkering") through the [Port of Fujairah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port%5Fof%5FFujairah "Port of Fujairah").[\[179\]](#cite%5Fnote-GL-186)

In April 2020, during the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19%5Fpandemic "COVID-19 pandemic"), the IRGC unveiled the [Mostaan 110](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostaan%5F110 "Mostaan 110"), an experimental medical device that the IRGC claimed could detect instances of [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19 "COVID-19") using electromagnetic radiation. The IRGC's claims of Mostaan 110's capabilities were met with widespread criticism from both Iranian and international experts, who called it [pseudoscientific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pseudoscience "Pseudoscience") and compared it to the [ADE 651](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ADE%5F651 "ADE 651"), a fake [explosive detector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Explosive%5Fdetection "Explosive detection") with a similar design.[\[180\]](#cite%5Fnote-187)[\[181\]](#cite%5Fnote-188)[\[182\]](#cite%5Fnote-189)[\[183\]](#cite%5Fnote-190)

In December 2022, German authorities accused the IRGC of attempting to orchestrate attacks against synagogues in the state of North Rhine-Westphalia and spying on the president of the Central Council of Jews in Germany.[\[184\]](#cite%5Fnote-191) Moreover, in 2020, IRGC members were exposed as having given a talk to UK students in which one spoke of an apocalyptic war with Jews.[\[185\]](#cite%5Fnote-192)

In July 2024, it was suspected that the IRGC had attempted to assassinate the former Iranian president, [Mahmoud Ahmadinejad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud%5FAhmadinejad "Mahmoud Ahmadinejad").[\[186\]](#cite%5Fnote-193)[\[187\]](#cite%5Fnote-194)

Following the [2026 Iran massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fmassacres "2026 Iran massacres"), the [Kaveh organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaveh%5F%28organization%29?action=edit&redlink=1 "Kaveh (organization) (page does not exist)") was founded to form resistance against IRGC members.[\[188\]](#cite%5Fnote-195)

### Terrorist designation and sanctions

See also: [List of designated terrorist groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List%5Fof%5Fdesignated%5Fterrorist%5Fgroups "List of designated terrorist groups")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/IRGC_terrorist_designation.svg/250px-IRGC_terrorist_designation.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:IRGC%5Fterrorist%5Fdesignation.svg)

Iran

Designated IRGC as a terrorist organization

Since 15 April 2019, the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates "United States"), which opposes the activities of Sepah, considers the IRGC as a [terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist%5Forganization "Terrorist organization"),[\[189\]](#cite%5Fnote-196) which some top [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central%5FIntelligence%5FAgency "Central Intelligence Agency") and [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FPentagon "The Pentagon") officials reportedly opposed.[\[190\]](#cite%5Fnote-Wong8April-197) On 8 April, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin%5FNetanyahu "Benjamin Netanyahu") tweeted in Hebrew that America's terrorist designation was the fulfillment of "another important request of mine."[\[191\]](#cite%5Fnote-Kershner9April-198) This designation was criticized by a number of governments including [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey"), [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq "Iraq") and [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China") as well as the [Islamic Consultative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FConsultative%5FAssembly "Islamic Consultative Assembly"), Iran's parliament, in which members wore IRGC uniforms in protest.[\[192\]](#cite%5Fnote-199)

On 29 April 2019, United States Deputy Assistant Secretary of Defense [Michael Mulroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael%5FMulroy "Michael Mulroy") said [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran") posed five threats. The first was Iran obtaining a nuclear weapon. The second was to maritime security in the [Straits of Hormuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straits%5Fof%5FHormuz "Straits of Hormuz") and the [Bab al-Mandab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bab%5Fal-Mandab "Bab al-Mandab"), because a substantial portion of energy trade and commercial goods go through those areas. The third was because of their support to proxies and militant organizations, including [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") in Lebanon and Syria, [Houthis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthis "Houthis") in Yemen, some [Hashd al-Shaabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashd%5Fal-Shaabi "Hashd al-Shaabi") in Iraq and safe-harboring senior [al-Qaeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda "Al-Qaeda") leaders in Iran. The fourth was Iranian made ballistic missiles sent to Houthi-controlled areas of Yemen for use against [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%5FArabia "Saudi Arabia") and to Syria with Hezbollah to use against [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel"). Cyber is the fifth threat and a growing concern.[\[193\]](#cite%5Fnote-200)[\[194\]](#cite%5Fnote-201)[\[195\]](#cite%5Fnote-c-span.org-202) He also said that the terrorist designation did not grant any additional authorities to the Department of Defense and that they were not asking for any.[\[195\]](#cite%5Fnote-c-span.org-202)

The IRGC has never been designated as a terrorist organization by the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FNations "United Nations"), although the [UNSCR 1929](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FNations%5FSecurity%5FCouncil%5FResolution%5F1929 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 1929") had its assets frozen (this was lifted in 2016). Since 2010, the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European%5FUnion "European Union") has imposed broad sanctions on the IRGC and many of its members, without designating it as a terrorist organization.[\[196\]](#cite%5Fnote-203)[\[197\]](#cite%5Fnote-204)

Although [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%5FArabia "Saudi Arabia") and [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain "Bahrain") already designated the IRGC as a terrorist organisation,[\[198\]](#cite%5Fnote-Terror-205)[\[199\]](#cite%5Fnote-wsj8apr-206) several countries such as [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia "Australia") are examining the possibility to designate the group as well. Canada outlawed the [Quds Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds%5FForce "Quds Force") in 2012.[\[200\]](#cite%5Fnote-207)[\[201\]](#cite%5Fnote-208) On 3 October 2022, in reaction to the [death of Mahsa Amini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death%5Fof%5FMahsa%5FAmini "Death of Mahsa Amini") and the persecution of protestors in the [protests that ensued](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa%5FAmini%5Fprotests "Mahsa Amini protests"), Canada officially sanctioned the IRGC. Foreign Affairs Minister [Mélanie Joly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mélanie%5FJoly "Mélanie Joly") announced sanctions targeting 9 entities, including the [Morality Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guidance%5FPatrol "Guidance Patrol") and the Iranian Ministry of Intelligence and Security, and 25 individuals, that include high-ranking officials and members of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps. These individuals include IRGC Commander-in-Chief Major General [Hossein Salami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein%5FSalami "Hossein Salami"), and [Esmail Qaani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esmail%5FQaani "Esmail Qaani"), commander of the Quds Force of the IRGC.[\[202\]](#cite%5Fnote-209)

On 7 October 2022, the [Canadian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian%5Fgovernment "Canadian government") expanded the sanctions, banning 10,000 members of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps from entering the country permanently, which represents the top 50% of the organization's leadership. Canadian Prime Minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin%5FTrudeau "Justin Trudeau") added that Canada plans to expand the sanctions against those most responsible for Iran's "egregious behavior". Canadian Deputy Prime Minister [Chrystia Freeland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrystia%5FFreeland "Chrystia Freeland") added that Iran was a "state sponsor of terror", and that "it is oppressive, theocratic and misogynist; The IRGC leadership are terrorists, the IRGC is a terrorist organization".[\[203\]](#cite%5Fnote-210)[\[204\]](#cite%5Fnote-211)

According to _[Arab News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%5FNews "Arab News")_, a 2020 report by the [Tony Blair Institute for Global Change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony%5FBlair%5FInstitute%5Ffor%5FGlobal%5FChange "Tony Blair Institute for Global Change") said that the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps is an "institutionalized militia" that "uses its vast resources to spread a 'mission of jihad' through an 'ideological army' of recruits and proxies".[\[205\]](#cite%5Fnote-212) In 2022, U.S. Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony%5FBlinken "Antony Blinken") said that the IRGC is "probably the most designated organisation – one way or another – in the world among the organisations that we designate, including the foreign terrorist organisation designation".[\[206\]](#cite%5Fnote-213)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Military_exercise_of_Heydar_Karar_Battalion_of_Fatehin_Unit_%2820%29.jpg/250px-Military_exercise_of_Heydar_Karar_Battalion_of_Fatehin_Unit_%2820%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Military%5Fexercise%5Fof%5FHeydar%5FKarar%5FBattalion%5Fof%5FFatehin%5FUnit%5F%2820%29.jpg)

Heydar Karar Battalion of the Fatehin Special Unit conducting training in Damavand, 4 September 2021

In January 2023, it was reported that the United Kingdom was preparing to declare the IRGC a terrorist organization; this did not subsequently occur.[\[207\]](#cite%5Fnote-214) On 18 January 2023, the [European Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European%5FParliament "European Parliament") passed an amendment proposed by the [ECR Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European%5FConservatives%5Fand%5FReformists%5FGroup "European Conservatives and Reformists Group"), to call for the EU and its member states to include the IRGC on the EU's terrorist list.[\[208\]](#cite%5Fnote-215)

On 19 June 2024, [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada "Canada") designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization under the nation's [Criminal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal%5FCode%5F%28Canada%29 "Criminal Code (Canada)"), citing "disregard for human rights" and "willingness to destabilize the international rules-based order."[\[30\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc20240619-34)

In March 2025, the United States reportedly requested the new [transitional administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian%5Fcaretaker%5Fgovernment "Syrian caretaker government") in [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria "Syria") to designate the IRGC as a terrorist organization as a condition for partial sanctions relief.[\[209\]](#cite%5Fnote-216) On 25 April 2025, [Paraguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraguay "Paraguay") designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[\[210\]](#cite%5Fnote-upi.com-217)

On 26 August 2025, [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia "Australia") indicated that it would designate the IRGC as a terrorist organization, pending legislation. This [followed attacks on Jewish sites in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%5FIranian%5Foperations%5Finside%5FAustralia "2024 Iranian operations inside Australia") which the [Australian Security Intelligence Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian%5FSecurity%5FIntelligence%5FOrganisation "Australian Security Intelligence Organisation") determined were directed by the IRGC.[\[211\]](#cite%5Fnote-abc.net.au20250826-218) On 16 September 2025, [Ecuador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecuador "Ecuador") designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization alongside Hamas and Hezbollah, citing threats to Ecuador's sovereignty and national security.[\[212\]](#cite%5Fnote-ecuadorlabelsirgc-219)

On 27 November 2025, the [Parliament of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament%5Fof%5FAustralia "Parliament of Australia") formally listed the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps as a [State Sponsor of Terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-sponsored%5Fterrorism "State-sponsored terrorism"), under the framework of the Criminal Code Amendment (State Sponsors of Terrorism) Act 2025.[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-220)[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-221) The IRGC was the first entity to be designated as such, after the Minister of Home Affairs determined that it met the criteria specified in Division 110 of the code, thereby following the recommendations of Australian Government intelligence, security and policy agencies.[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-220)[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-221) According to the listing, it is "illegal to direct the activities of, be a member of, associate with members of, recruit for, train with, get funds to, from or for, or provide support to, the Iranian Revolutionary Guard Corp",[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-220)[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-221) with a penalty of 25 years of imprisonment.[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-220)[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-221)

On 17 January 2026, Argentina designated the IRGC Quds Force as terrorist organization. The decree was signed by President [Javier Milei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier%5FMilei "Javier Milei").[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-222) On 22 January 2026, the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European%5FUnion "European Union") called for the designation of the IRGC as a terrorist organization and advocated for further sanctions, in response to [Iran's violent crackdown and massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fmassacres "2026 Iran massacres") against the [2025–2026 Iranian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025–2026%5FIranian%5Fprotests "2025–2026 Iranian protests").[\[216\]](#cite%5Fnote-223) A few days later, [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France "France") said it would back the designation, marking a reversal of its earlier position.[\[217\]](#cite%5Fnote-224) On 29 January 2026, the EU designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[\[218\]](#cite%5Fnote-LBCIV7-225) Following the European Union's decision to proscribe the IRGC, the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FKingdom "United Kingdom") is set to follow suit.[\[219\]](#cite%5Fnote-:2-226) [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine") also followed suit on 2 February 2026, citing Iran's crackdown on protestors and [Iranian support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%5Fand%5Fthe%5FRusso-Ukrainian%5Fwar%5F%282022–present%29 "Iran and the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)") for the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Ukrainian%5Fwar%5F%282022–present%29 "Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)") by providing [Shahed drones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahed%5Fdrones "Shahed drones").[\[32\]](#cite%5Fnote-Ukraine-36) Eight European countries including [Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania "Albania"), [Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnia%5Fand%5FHerzegovina "Bosnia and Herzegovina"), [Iceland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iceland "Iceland"), [Liechtenstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liechtenstein "Liechtenstein"), [Moldova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moldova "Moldova"), [Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montenegro "Montenegro"), [North Macedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North%5FMacedonia "North Macedonia"), and [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia "Serbia") followed suit in mid-March 2026 aligning with the EU decisions after consultations with the Israeli foreign ministry.[\[220\]](#cite%5Fnote-six-227)[\[221\]](#cite%5Fnote-Balkans-228)

On 5 March 2026, [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon") banned the IRGC from operating within its territory, and stated that its members would face deportation.[\[222\]](#cite%5Fnote-229) On 1 April 2026, Argentina designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[\[27\]](#cite%5Fnote-auto-31) Local officials said that this measure follows the IRGC’s alleged links to militant group [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah"), which Argentina accuses of carrying out the 1992 Israeli embassy bombing in Buenos Aires (killing 29 and injuring more than 200 individuals), and the 1994 bombing of the [Asociacion Mutual Israelita Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asociación%5FMutual%5FIsraelita%5FArgentina "Asociación Mutual Israelita Argentina") Jewish community center- the deadliest terrorist attack in Argentina’s history.[\[223\]](#cite%5Fnote-230)[\[224\]](#cite%5Fnote-231)

On 8 April 2026, [Costa Rica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa%5FRica "Costa Rica") designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[\[225\]](#cite%5Fnote-CostaRica-232) On 15 April 2026, [Trinidad and Tobago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinidad%5Fand%5FTobago "Trinidad and Tobago") designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[\[226\]](#cite%5Fnote-233) On 24 April 2026, Prime Minister of United Kingdom [Keir Starmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keir%5FStarmer "Keir Starmer") promised in an interview with the [_Jewish Chronicle_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FJewish%5FChronicle "The Jewish Chronicle") to proscribe the IRGC by July 2026, which was then done using the new powers granted by the [National Security (State Threats) Act 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National%5FSecurity%5F%28State%5FThreats%29%5FAct%5F2026 "National Security (State Threats) Act 2026").[\[227\]](#cite%5Fnote-234)[\[228\]](#cite%5Fnote-235)

### Response to terrorist organization designation

The move was met with unfavorable reactions from Iranian leaders and militants.[\[229\]](#cite%5Fnote-236) Shortly after the US announced the designation, the Iranian government declared the United States Central Command, whose area of responsibility includes the Middle East, as a terrorist organization.[\[230\]](#cite%5Fnote-wsj20190408-237) According to Iran's Supreme National Security Council, the move "was in response to the illegal and unwise move from the U.S."[\[230\]](#cite%5Fnote-wsj20190408-237) The next day, Iranian Members of Parliament displayed their support of the IRGC by collectively wearing green military pants and chanted "[death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death%5Fto%5FAmerica "Death to America")" as they opened session. Iranian president Hassan Rouhani also responded to the move, commenting that it was a mistake which would only increase the IRGC's popularity in Iran and elsewhere.[\[230\]](#cite%5Fnote-wsj20190408-237)

Since the designation, the [United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FDepartment%5Fof%5FState "United States Department of State")'s [Rewards for Justice Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rewards%5Ffor%5FJustice%5FProgram "Rewards for Justice Program") has offered a reward of up to US$15 million for financial background information about the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps and its branches,[\[231\]](#cite%5Fnote-238) including an IRGC financier, [Abdul Reza Shahlai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul%5FReza%5FShahlai "Abdul Reza Shahlai"),[\[g\]](#cite%5Fnote-241) who it says was responsible for a raid that killed five American soldiers in [Karbala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karbala "Karbala"), Iraq on 20 January 2007.[\[234\]](#cite%5Fnote-242)

Following Canada's designation of the IRGC as a terrorist organization, Iran strongly condemned the move. As a result, the Foreign Ministry summoned the Italian ambassador in Tehran, who represents Canadian interests in the country. In a statement, the ministry expressed Iran's "strong protest" over what it described as an "unlawful and internationally illegal" act by the Canadian government. The statement also warned of potential consequences and emphasized Iran's right to take necessary and reciprocal measures. According to the ministry, the Italian ambassador pledged to promptly convey the message to Canadian authorities.[\[235\]](#cite%5Fnote-243) Spokesperson [Nasser Kanaani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser%5FKanaani "Nasser Kanaani") called the move "hostile" and contrary to international law while the acting foreign minister [Ali Bagheri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FBagheri "Ali Bagheri") said on [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter "Twitter") that the Canadian government will be responsible for the consequences of this provocative and irresponsible decision, referring to the designation.[\[236\]](#cite%5Fnote-244)
